## 19. Step D screens (rules 37-54), one line each
- **35 constitution:** loaded before the first battery; §§ 1, 7, 8, 10, 59 screened; § 7 (jury) and § 1 (arms) reach rows (`edu-jury-waiver-ky`, `edu-firearms-ky`).
- **37 tenancy type:** week-to-week, month-to-month, fixed-term and holdover tenancies differ in both tiers (KRS 383.565(3), 383.695(1)-(4), 383.160, 383.195); the protective-order rules turn on the lease date (June 29, 2017); stated in `edu-termination-notice-periods-ky`, `edu-tenancy-at-will-ky`, `holdover-ky`.
- **38 no imported structure:** the URLTA's Kentucky text was read against each shared clause; Kentucky's 15-day cure, 10-day holdover notice, no-rent-reduction casualty text and conjunctive deposit forfeiture are its own (§6.3).
- **39 eviction duties:** no post-writ storage duty (`edu-post-eviction-property-ky`); no self-help (`edu-self-help-eviction-ky`); no sealing (`edu-eviction-records-ky`); the appeal rent deposit (`edu-eviction-process-ky`); the squatter procedure excludes former tenants (KRS 383.290(9)).
- **40 formatting and placement:** fmt-r2, blank-spaces, copy-of-lease; layout table §4.
- **41 just cause:** none; recorded in `edu-no-for-cause-eviction-ky` (topic `for-cause-eviction`).
- **42 required text in a shared clause:** the deposit account and number (`security-deposit-use-ky`), the manager and owner disclosure (`landlord-disclosure-ky`), the lien-exemption sentence (`no-liens-ky`); each is its own clause.
- **43 cure promises:** `default-by-tenant-ky` gives 7 and 15 days and keeps the six-month no-cure repeat in its own sentence; `early-termination` (10-day cure for any breach) not tagged.
- **44 terms turned into duties:** landlord-supplied appliances and facilities must be maintained (KRS 383.595(1)(d), 'supplied or required to be supplied'); utilities the law requires the landlord to supply (`utilities-responsibility-ky`).
- **45 electronic notices:** KRS 369.103 (exclusions: wills, most UCC, negotiable instruments; no lease or notice exclusion) and 369.105 (agreement to transact electronically; non-waivable refusal right) read; termination notices follow KRS 383.560 where the URLTA applies; `electronic-signatures` tagged; `edu-notice-delivery-ky`.
- **46 lease as the notice:** the owner disclosure, deposit account and lien-exemption term can be lease paragraphs; the damage lists and the chore agreement cannot (§4).
- **47 knowing-use penalties:** KRS 383.570 has none; KRS 383.302(2) does for police-call terms (`edu-knowing-use-penalty-ky`); no Kentucky debt-collection statute adds one (debt-collection, collection-costs-r2).
- **48 separate documents:** KRS 383.595(4)(a) (chores) → `maintenance-allocation-ky`; KRS 371.065(1) (separate guaranty); no other separate-writing rule for leases.
- **49 collection costs:** KRS 383.570(1)(c) where the URLTA applies; KRS 411.195 elsewhere (`attorney-fees-non-urlta-ky`); collection and notice-service fees not offered.
- **50 'the lease controls':** each choice made on purpose: rent time and place (KRS 383.565(2)), use as a dwelling (KRS 383.620), the casualty rent rule ('unless he otherwise contracts', KRS 383.170: no contrary term), the repair-covenant casualty rule (KRS 383.170: none), the deposit-on-sale rule ('Unless otherwise agreed', KRS 383.600: none), the 15-day cure (lease gives it), waiver by acceptance (KRS 383.675: no pre-breach agreement possible).
- **51 plain language and consumer contracts:** no plain-language statute (fmt-r2, blank-spaces); consumer protection in `edu-consumer-protection-ky`, unconscionability in `edu-unconscionability-ky`.
- **52 exculpation:** KRS 383.570(1)(d) where the URLTA applies; the ks-oh and ks-oh-ca variants tagged; `pet-policy-ky` without the indemnity; `edu-exculpatory-clauses-ky`.
- **53 figures vs shared clauses:** `landlords-access` (24 hours), `possession-delay` (30 days), `holdover` and `returned-payments` (ceiling-only) replaced; `late-fee` (anti-waiver) replaced.
- **54 optional clauses:** §6.1. **54t tenant-caused damage:** `tenant-caused-damage-ky`.
- **79 summaries re-read:** every row written section-open; qualifiers attached to their own sentences; six independent-check rounds and a check of this log (§13). No KY clause lacks a basis.

## Proposed SOP changes
1. Rule 19: before citing a battery for an absence, also search the regime's own nouns with no tenancy context limb (the object of the rule: 'money belonging to others', 'personalty', 'solid waste', 'registrant', 'pool', 'unfit ... for human habitation', the federal act's name); Kentucky's independent check found ten false or overbroad absences whose batteries required a tenancy word the controlling statute never used.
2. Rule 26: the tag-first table must have a reason for every TAG row, and the assembler should refuse an empty one; 23 Kentucky tag notes went to the first check with an empty reason because the screen table's cell was blank.
3. Rule 16: a bill's status may support a row only if the bill page is saved and hashed like any other source; Kentucky's early rows cited 2026 bill statuses read in the browser but not saved, and the check removed them.
4. Two-tier states (local adoption of the uniform act, as in Kentucky and Tennessee): fix a scope-label set before drafting and define it once (for clauses: where used; for education rows: BOTH TIERS when both statewide and act-only rules are stated); Kentucky's labels drifted until round 2.
5. Rule 14: in the built-in browser, downloads land under temporary `.tmp` names, and a download click reloaded the crawl tab once; export from a second tab on the same origin and identify files by size and SHA-256.
6. Rule 59: the quote checker should resolve a citation list ('KRS 344.360, 344.365(4)') to every section in it, not only the first; Kentucky's first checker flagged correct quotes for that reason.

## Proposed topic questions
1. `guarantor-renewal`: Does a general guaranty statute require a separate guaranty to state a maximum liability and an end date unless it is written on or refers to the lease? (Kentucky, KRS 371.065.)
2. `construction-liens`: Can the lease itself keep the lessor's interest free of liens for tenant improvements if it says so expressly and the tenant tells contractors? (Kentucky, KRS 376.010(3), leases on or after June 29, 2023.)
3. `landlord-maintenance`: Outside the landlord-tenant act, does a local-government statute make every owner keep structures fit for habitation, enforced by code fines and liens? (Kentucky, KRS 65.8840(5).)
4. `abandoned-property`: Is there a separate lien and sale procedure for property abandoned on a rented mobile home lot? (Kentucky, KRS 376.480.)
5. `servicemember-rights`: Does a state statute extend the federal SCRA's protections to National Guard members on state active duty? (Kentucky, KRS 38.510.)
6. `municipal-utility-lien`: Can a local government put a tenant's unpaid garbage fees on the owner's property tax bill, and how does a non-occupying owner avoid it? (Kentucky, KRS 109.310.)
7. `pool-safety`: Do health statutes class a residential community's pool as a limited-access pool with lifeguard and shut-off duties? (Kentucky, KRS 211.203.)
8. `meth-disclosure`: Does the penalty for leasing without the disclosure turn on a contamination determination rather than the posted notice that triggers the disclosure duty? (Kentucky, KRS 224.99-010(15), 224.1-410.)
9. `scope`: Where the uniform act applies only by local adoption, which localities have adopted it, and is there a primary list? (Kentucky, KRS 383.500.)

## Rule type: early termination (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-12: `early-termination-ks` is now `rule_type` RECOMMENDED, not REQUIRED, with the shared `early-termination-ks` and every state version that copied its label (OH, NV, TX, NJ, AZ, GA, SC, NC, VA, PA, IL). Under SOP rule 56, REQUIRED means a statute requires the clause in the lease, and no state's does. The label is research metadata only; no clause text changed. Taylor's lesson 2 (flag a lease without an early-termination clause) stays as a builder warning in the backlog.

## Federal row: HUD assistance-animal enforcement change (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-12: the assistance-animal clause stays as written (an animal a tenant needs for a disability, trained or not, is an accommodation, not a pet), and a new federal education row, `edu-assistance-animals-federal-enforcement`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction but CO. It explains HUD's September 17, 2025 withdrawal of its assistance-animal guidance (91 Fed. Reg. 17291) and FHEO's May 22, 2026 memorandum limiting Fair Housing Act charges to trained animals, and what neither changes: the Act, 24 C.F.R. § 100.204, private suits, Section 504 and ADA complaints, and state law. No clause text changed.

### §1 Sources, channel and currency

- **Step A (2026-11-19):** `lease-clauses.csv` decompressed from the attached `.xz`: 76,632,518 bytes, SHA-256 `630cd8ec36c6141e7cad6b36733949bc2b1e08d703d4ada39c0be7af053ce2a0` (matches); 19,062 rows, 18,950 active, 223 active rows carrying KY (match); `missing-topics-KY.md` lists 154 topics (match); `lease-clause-decision-log-KY.md` attached. Earlier outputs in `/mnt/user-data/outputs/` deleted.
- **Corpus:** `corpus-KY-20261119` (four parts, each SHA-256 and size matched the prompt before unpacking); `python3 -I verify_ky.py` reported 5,329 files checked, 0 mismatches, 0 unlisted; 25,272 KRS section entries; 1,247 kept PDFs checked, 0 mismatches. README read first. Holdings: the whole KRS (every chapter page: "Includes enactments through the 2026 Regular Session") with each section's Effective date and History line; KAR titles 815, 807, 902, 401, 104 and 40 whole plus 201 KAR 11 (only versions in force are searched or cited: 730 regulations with status Current or Emergency; 1,401 repealed, inactive, expired, withdrawn, recodified, proposed or pending-amendment entries excluded); the Constitution (277 units); the 2025 and 2026 Regular Session Acts (split by chapter and proved complete against `acts_table_by_chapter.html`: 2025 chs. 1-162, 2026 chs. 1-202); forms AOC-215, 216, 217, 218 and 220. Not held: court rules, case law, local ordinances, the model codes adopted by reference, federal law.
- **KAR loader correction (2026-11-20, during batch 2):** each saved regulation page prints the engrossed text in force (ending with its HISTORY line) followed by an "ALTERNATE VIEW" of how it appeared before it was engrossed, and 48 entries the chapter pages listed as Emergency are, on their own pages, expired, inactive, superseded or withdrawn ("This document is no longer current", page banner) or hold no regulation text. `kycorp.py` now keeps only the engrossed text and drops those 48, leaving 682 regulations in force. Batteries KYG-1A-*, KYG-1B-*, KYG-2B-001 to 012 and the first KYG-2A batteries ran before the fix over 730 units including the alternate views (their citations say "KAR 730"): a superset, so no absence is weakened; a script re-tested every KAR hit those batteries record against the corrected text, and the only hits that fall away are in KYG-1B-006 (seven empty emergency-page entries; not cited by any row) and KYG-2B-008 (not cited). Every quotation in every topic file was re-checked against the corrected text and still matches.
- **Channel:** every Kentucky quotation comes from the corpus (`text_raw`), searched by `work/kycorp.py` over both `text_eol_joined` and `text_raw` (rule 19 [WI-gaps 1, 2]). Federal text was read in the built-in browser on uscode.house.gov ("Text contains those laws in effect through Public Law 119-142 (November 10, 2026)", page banner) and saved to `work/federal/`: FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955; FEDERAL: 11 U.S.C. § 362(a), (b)(22)-(23), (k)(1), (l), (m); FEDERAL: 34 U.S.C. § 12491(b). One court rule was read on govt.westlaw.com/kyrules in the built-in browser, one page at a time, and saved to `work/courtrules/`: SCR 3.020 (court rule; page banner "Current with amendments received through November 1, 2026"). The Civil Rules, the District Court and small claims rules were not read; rows that would turn on them say so. No bot block or CAPTCHA was met.
- **Currency (rule 16):** the corpus and the first pass both state enactments through the 2026 Regular Session; the corpus's session-law folder holds no extraordinary-session acts, so nothing has been enacted since the first pass. A script listed every 2025 and 2026 act section amending or creating a KRS section in the chapters the missing topics rely on: none amends chapter 383. Relevant amendments, each confirmed in the compiled KRS History line with its effective date: KRS 65.111 (2025 Ky. Acts ch. 56, sec. 7, effective June 27, 2025); KRS 383.290 (created 2025 Ky. Acts ch. 63, sec. 1, effective June 27, 2025); KRS 511.010 and 511.080 (2026 Ky. Acts ch. 67, effective July 15, 2026); KRS 416.550 (2026 Ky. Acts ch. 112, sec. 3, emergency, effective April 13, 2026); KRS 382.110 and 392.020 (effective July 15, 2026); KRS 65.8840 (effective April 14, 2026); KRS 198B.030 (2026 Ky. Acts ch. 178, emergency, effective April 16, 2026); KRS 381.800 (2025 Ky. Acts ch. 32, sec. 1, effective June 27, 2025); KRS 100.348 (2025 Ky. Acts ch. 154, sec. 1, effective July 1, 2026). Acts without an emergency clause take effect ninety days after adjournment (Ky. Const. § 55): June 27, 2025 and July 15, 2026. Future-dated versions the corpus prints as separate entries (for example KRS 367.3611 (effective July 1, 2027), 2026 Ky. Acts ch. 118; the new chapter 367 consumer data regime of 2026 Ky. Acts ch. 16, effective January 1, 2027) are legal-watch items; any row relying on one names the version.

### §2 Topics answered

One line each: topic, row id, status, citation. Status words: Present (Kentucky law answers it), Absent (confirmed absence, battery in §3), Pointer (answered by an existing KY row under another key; this row points to it), Barred, Declined option (rule 54), Tagged.

### §3 Batteries for every absence

Every battery was run by `kycorp.battery` over the whole corpus (KRS 25,272 section entries, the KAR regulations in force in the titles held (730 units before the loader correction in §1, 682 after), 277 Constitution units, 364 session-law chapters, 5 forms) unless the line says otherwise, with control term "zqxjvwk" (0 hits everywhere) and at least one positive that passed; failed batteries are not cited. Citations below are generated from the saved battery logs (`work/batteries/KYG-*.jsonl`). Boundary for every absence: KAR titles other than 815, 807, 902, 401, 104, 40 and 201 KAR 11, case law, local ordinances, court rules (except where §1 says one was read) and the model codes adopted by reference were not searched.

### §4 Decisions and questions asked of Taylor

- **Questions asked of Taylor: none.** Every point was settled from the sources, by the cautious course where only case law could settle it (SOP Part 5), or by the lead under rule 76; the answers to the helpers' questions are listed below.
- **Item 9(b), casualty fault persons (confirmed, no change proposed).** KRS 383.625(1)(c), 383.635(2) and 383.640(4) each name 'the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of his family, or other person on the premises with his consent' (KRS 383.625(1)(c)); `tenant-caused-damage-ky` names the same three persons ("Tenant, a member of Tenant's family, or any other person on the premises with Tenant's consent"). KRS 383.650 names no fault persons and lets either party terminate after a casualty whoever caused it; `casualty-ky` tracks it (either party, 14 days, tenant may vacate immediately, prepaid rent returned and accounted as of the casualty) and leaves fault to the damage clause ("damage Tenant causes, as this Lease's Damage Caused by Tenant terms provide"), which keeps the tenant's termination right and adds only damages. Outside URLTA localities KRS 383.170 turns on the tenant's own fault or neglect but applies 'unless he otherwise contracts' (KRS 383.170), so the lease may extend rent liability to family members and consenting guests; both clauses fit all five sections.
- **Item 9(c), CARES (confirmed).** No KY eviction row contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`. `edu-nonpayment-notice-ky`, `edu-cure-periods-ky`, `edu-termination-notice-periods-ky`, `edu-eviction-process-ky`, `edu-expedited-criminal-eviction-ky`, `default-by-tenant-ky` and `criminal-activity-ky` state Kentucky's own notice periods (seven days for rent, 14 or 15 days for other breaches, the holdover and periodic notices) and none says a state notice is enough for a federally covered property; `edu-cares-act-notice` expressly adds its 30-day notice on top of the state's notices. The nearest wording is `edu-termination-notice-periods-ky`'s holdover sentence ("the landlord may end it at any time without notice"), which states the state rule only. Optional at sync, not a defect: add `edu-cares-act-notice` as a pointer in `edu-eviction-process-ky`'s last sentence.
- **Tags checked by the lead (batch 3A):** `cold-weather-vacate-notice-wy` (an absence-notice term shorter than the seven days KRS 383.620 lets a lease require is a contract term, not a waiver of a tenant right; its heat and freeze-damage terms fit KRS 383.595(1)(e) and 383.605 and carve out heat the landlord must supply), `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` (optional, fixed Term, court process only; the retaliation and protective-order cautions are in `edu-no-owner-move-in-rule-ky`) and `barred-person-covenant-ne` (its protective-order and police-call carve-outs cover KRS 383.300 and 383.302). Each stays as tagged.
- **New lease clauses signed off by the lead (batch 4A):** `utility-transfer-ky` (SERVES_LANDLORD), `fire-safety-compliance-ky` (SERVES_LANDLORD; its 14-day notice-and-bill route follows KRS 383.665 where the URLTA applies and is a contract term elsewhere) and `common-area-lighting-ky` (CONSTRAINED_TERM; existing variable `{{tenant_metered_common_areas}}`). No new variables.
- **Builder note (product work, not a row):** 104 KAR 1:010 requires the Human Rights Commission's Fair Housing Brochure at rental (`edu-rights-responsibilities-guide-ky`); the lease builder could add a tenant acknowledgment of receipt for Kentucky.
- **New lease clauses signed off by the lead (batch 5):** `home-business-permission-ky` (SERVES_LANDLORD; Kentucky copy of `home-business-permission-co` without the additional-insured term and with the `tenant-caused-damage-ky` fault wording) and `association-documents-delivery-ky` (CONDITIONAL, SERVES_LANDLORD; where the URLTA applies, a later association rule binds the tenant under the lease only as KRS 383.610 allows). Tag declines on the KS association clause and the CO and MS hunting clauses follow the cautious reading of KRS 383.570(1)(d) and 383.615(4).

Tenant will not attorn to, or pay rent to, anyone claiming the property other than Landlord without Landlord's written consent, except as a court orders or after Tenant receives notice that Landlord's interest has been sold or conveyed." The carve-out follows KRS 383.100(1)-(2). Where the URLTA applies, damages for breach would come under KRS 383.660(3); no liquidated sum or penalty should be added. If the lead offers it, the body's last two sentences change to point to the clause. **Lead:** Declined (rule 54): no statute calls for it and the existing row tells the landlord they can add their own clause; the library offers no new adverse-proceeding clause in Kentucky (the ND clause stays untagged).

### §5 Independent check

- **Method (SOP rule 80, scaled down):** two rounds, one checking agent at a time, none of which wrote the rows. Round 1 covered every new row and tag in four sequential parts (A1, A2: the 72 topics with clauses, tags or present-law rows; B1, B2: the 82 confirmed-absence topics). Each checker opened every relied-on section whole, checked each statement of law, the tier, clause lawfulness and pointers, and for absences ran its own differently worded greps; it proposed exact old-to-new fixes and edited nothing. The lead reviewed and applied the fixes by script (each old string had to occur once), saving every edited row's previous version. Round 2 re-checked only the rows edited after round 1, diffing each against its saved previous version.
- **Round 1:** A1 0 errors, 12 improvements (36 topics); A2 8 errors, 23 improvements (36); B1 9 errors, 17 improvements (41); B2 12 errors, 6 improvements (41). All accepted and applied (90 edits on 63 rows, plus two lead follow-ups from the checkers' notes: a leftover spelling in `home-business-permission-ky`, and `common-area-lighting-ky` moved from CONSTRAINED_TERM to SERVES_LANDLORD with its notes aligned). Substantive errors fixed: KRS 382.010 (a lease over one year is conveyed by deed) missed in `edu-lease-term-limitation-ky`; KRS 383.180(2) and KRS 383.170 written as non-URLTA only although they have no locality limit; KRS 383.595(4)(a) (separate signed maintenance writing) missed in two absence rows; KRS 381.800 (political yard signs in planned communities), KRS 324.020 and 324.045(5) (licensed property managers, FBI check) and 201 KAR 11:121, Section 10(4)-(6) (broker escrow and receipts) missed in absence rows; KRS 72.020 misdescribed; plus several statutory conditions dropped in paraphrase and backticked topic keys. Four topics' status changed to "Absent / Present in part" (§2).
- **Round 2:** 63 edited rows re-checked against their previous versions: 8 errors and 18 improvements on 18 ids; all applied. The main error: the round-1 wording that a landlord may recover possession after 10 days' notice to quit for an unconsented assignment "in every locality" overlooked that where the URLTA applies a landlord 'may not recover or take possession of the dwelling unit by action or otherwise' except as the Act permits (KRS 383.690); the three rows now keep the 10-day route outside URLTA localities and point to the Act's breach notice (KRS 383.660(1)) inside them. Others: `edu-landlord-remedies-termination-ky` tiers restored after a round-1 edit (rent lien unenforceable where the URLTA applies, KRS 383.680(1)); `edu-confirmed-absences-outside-title-ky` closing sentence and notes aligned with the broker receipt rule; one quote placement in `edu-tenant-records-ky`. `kyg_check.py all` after round 2: 154 files, 0 errors.
- **Rows edited after the last round (round 2), not re-checked by a third round:** `edu-confirmed-absences-misc-ky`, `edu-confirmed-absences-outside-title-ky`, `edu-landlord-remedies-termination-ky`, `edu-lease-registration-ky`, `edu-lease-term-limitation-ky`, `edu-no-condemnation-disclosure-ky`, `edu-no-designated-repairer-ky`, `edu-no-disaster-tenancy-rule-ky`, `edu-no-environmental-event-termination-ky`, `edu-no-forfeiture-relief-ky`, `edu-no-lease-initials-requirement-ky`, `edu-no-notice-to-vacate-terms-rule-ky`, `edu-no-political-access-rule-ky`, `edu-no-rent-demand-bar-ky`, `edu-no-repair-cost-termination-ky`, `edu-notice-to-quit-waiver-ky`, `edu-statutory-caps-ky`, `edu-tenant-records-ky`, `edu-trespass-notice-ky`, `edu-written-notice-required-ky`. If Tenant gives that notice, pays the fee and delivers possession of the property by the termination date, Tenant owes no Rent for any period after the termination date. If Tenant leaves before the end of the Term without using this option, Tenant's liability is governed by this Lease's Default by Tenant terms and Kentucky law, and Landlord will use reasonable efforts to re-rent the property at a fair rental. This early-termination option and fee apply only if this Lease has a fixed Term; a month-to-month or other periodic tenancy ends on the notice that Kentucky law and this Lease provide, without an early termination fee. Landlord may terminate this Lease early only as this Lease's Default by Tenant terms and Kentucky law allow, and will regain possession only through a court proceeding. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law. No early termination fee is owed when Tenant ends this Lease, or is released from it, under such a right. These rights include a protected tenant's right to terminate under Kentucky's protective-order law; release from this Lease because of a lead hazard to a young child; Tenant's right to terminate because of military service under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act; and Tenant's right to terminate after a fire or casualty, or because Landlord has not complied with this Lease or applicable law.",

- **Propagation for item 9(a):** when `early-termination-ky` is added, change the pointer in this pass's `edu-no-liquidated-damages-rule-ky` from `early-termination-ks` to `early-termination-ky` (its sentence "The library's early termination clause for Kentucky is `early-termination-ks`" is true until sync).

Optional at sync.
- **`edu-statute-of-frauds-ky` (found in the independent check):** the row says a lease over one year must be "in writing and signed by the party to be charged" (row text), but the conveyancing statute says no estate 'for a term of more than one (1) year, in real property shall be conveyed, except by deed or will' (KRS 382.010). Proposed added sentence: "A lease for more than one year is also a conveyance that must be made by deed (KRS 382.010); what that requires beyond a signed writing is case law, so have the landlord sign a written lease for any term over a year." This pass's `edu-lease-term-limitation-ky` and `edu-lease-registration-ky` now state the rule.
- **`edu-rent-receipts-ky` (found in the independent check):** a licensed broker managing property must give receipts: 'A receipt shall be given for money received' (201 KAR 11:121, Section 10(6)); the row searched no KAR and does not say so. Proposed added sentence: "If a licensed real estate broker manages the property, the broker must give a receipt for money received (201 KAR 11:121, Section 10(6))."
- (from `alarm-tampering-fee`) `edu-alarm-duties-ky` says "Kentucky Building Code and Fire Code (815 KAR 7, 815 KAR 10) not read (KY log §7)" and "its standards, set by a regulation not read here, must include smoke detectors" (row notes and text). Both are now in the corpus: the boarding home regulation requires 'Having smoke detectors located as follows: (a) One (1) shall be located in each bedroom; and (b) At least one (1) shall be located on each floor or level of the home' (902 KAR 20:350); and the Kentucky Standards of Safety apply to 'all buildings except one (1) and two (2) family dwellings' (815 KAR 10:060, Section 2(1)), adopt NFPA 1 (2018) by reference (Section 3(4)(c)), and let an owner test single-station smoke detectors, with 'Battery powered smoke alarms shall be tested weekly. A log of the test results shall be kept on site for review by the fire code official' (815 KAR 10:060, Section 7(2)(c)). Recommend the lead have `edu-alarm-duties-ky` (or a new row) say that a landlord of a building other than a one- or two-family dwelling is subject to the Standards of Safety, and check whether the weekly-test log rule reaches smoke alarms inside apartments (it sits in the owner-testing exception; NFPA 72 not held).
- (from `association-obligations-disclosure`) `edu-hoa-ky` says "Older condominiums fall under the Horizontal Property Law" and its notes say KRS 381.805 to 381.910 was "not read". That is incomplete: KRS 381.9103(2) makes KRS 381.9109, 381.9111, 381.9113, 381.9129, 381.9131, 381.9167, 381.9169, 381.9183, 381.9193, 381.9197, 381.9201(2), and 381.9203 'apply to all condominiums created before January 1, 2011, but only to the extent of events or circumstances occurring after January 1, 2011' (KRS 381.9103(2)), so the fines power (KRS 381.9167(1)(k)) and the resale certificate (KRS 381.9203) reach older condominiums. Suggested fix at the next KY sync: add one sentence, "Some sections of the 2011 Condominium Act, including the association's powers and the resale certificate, also apply to older condominiums for events after January 1, 2011 (KRS 381.9103(2))." `edu-association-obligations-disclosure-ky` states it meanwhile.
- (from `association-obligations-disclosure`) `edu-no-political-access-rule-ky` (this pass, batch 1B, topic `political-access`) does not mention KRS 381.800: 'The governing documents of an association shall not prohibit the outdoor display of political yard signs by an owner or resident on the owner’s or resident’s property' (KRS 381.800(1)), effective June 27, 2025, with contrary planned-community provisions void (KRS 381.800(2)). It binds a planned-community association, not a landlord, so the row's statement that no statute gives tenants a right to post political signs despite the lease stays true; the lead may add a pointer sentence. `edu-association-obligations-disclosure-ky` states the rule.
- (from `environmental-event-termination`) `edu-local-preemption-ky` says: "no Kentucky statute caps the amount or frequency of rent increases". KRS 367.374(1)(b) limits rent for housing during an implemented emergency: 'No person shall sell, rent, or offer to sell or rent, regardless of whether an actual sale or rental occurs, a good or service listed in this paragraph or any repair or reconstruction service for a price which is grossly in excess of the price prior to the declaration and unrelated to any increased cost to the seller.' (KRS 367.374(1)(b)), with '7. Housing' (KRS 367.374(1)(b)) and "Housing" 'means any rental housing and includes any housing provided by a hotel or motel' (KRS 367.372(7)). It applies only when the Governor implements it by executive order for the declared area, for 15 days with up to three 15-day extensions (KRS 367.374(1)(a), (2)); a price 'Ten percent (10%) or less above the price prior to the declaration' does not violate it (KRS 367.374(1)(c)). Proposed fix for the lead: add after that sentence "The one exception is a declared emergency: once the Governor implements Kentucky's price-gouging law by order, you may not rent housing in the declared area for a price grossly in excess of the price before the declaration, unless a statutory exception applies, such as an increase of 10% or less (KRS 367.374)." The row's batteries (rent-control, rent-escalation-r2) searched rent-increase words and could not match the price-gouging vocabulary (grossly in excess, Housing in a goods list). The same gap may affect `edu-rent-increase-notice-ky` ("No Kentucky statute sets a notice period for a rent increase" is unaffected; its cap statements, if any, are not).
- (from `ev-charging-requirements`) `edu-ev-charging-ky` says only "No Kentucky statute gives tenants a right to install an electric vehicle charger. Any charger needs the landlord's consent under the lease's alterations terms." (row text). It does not mention the electric vehicle power tax on a landlord who is an electric vehicle power dealer ('Receives payment for charges for power distributed through an electric vehicle charging station' (KRS 138.477(1)(d))) or the retail charging license required from January 1, 2027 (KRS 363.785(1)). The new `edu-ev-charging-requirements-ky` states both, so no change to the existing row is needed; recorded so the lead knows the existing row is narrower.
- (from `eviction-service-party`) `edu-dv-confidentiality-ky` says: "No Kentucky statute requires a landlord to keep a tenant's protective-order or victim information confidential." But KRS 14.304(9) binds any person a Safe at Home participant notifies in writing: 'if a program participant has notified a person in writing that he or she is a program participant, that person shall not knowingly disclose the program participant's: (a) Name; (b) Home address; (c) Work address; or (d) School address' (KRS 14.304(9)), with exceptions where 'that person also lives, works, or goes to school at the address disclosed, or the participant has provided written consent to disclosure' (KRS 14.304(10)). Program participants include victims of domestic violence and abuse, stalking, sex crimes and human trafficking (KRS 14.300(9)). The row's absence statement is wrong for a landlord a participant has notified in writing. Proposed fix (for the lead to apply; the row is not edited here): replace the first sentence with "No Kentucky statute requires a landlord to keep a tenant's protective-order information confidential, with one exception: if a tenant tells you in writing that they are in the Secretary of State's Safe at Home address-confidentiality program, you must not knowingly disclose their name or home, work or school address, unless they consent in writing or you live, work or go to school at that address (KRS 14.304(9)-(10))." and add to notes the KRS 14.304(9) quote with Read whole: KRS 14.300, 14.304 (both versions). The row's battery (dv-confid-r2) searched victim confidentiality in housing words and could not match the program's own vocabulary (rule 19 [WI 2], [FL-gaps 3]).

- (from `lead-state-notices`) `edu-lead-hazard-release-ky` (topic `lead-based-paint`) and `edu-condemned-premises-ky` state KRS 211.905 but not the local-program reletting bar or the KRS 211.994 fine. (902 KAR 4:090, Section 4(3)); the penalty: 'If the owner of the posted dwelling unit permits occupancy of the dwelling or dwelling unit, after the expiration of the thirty (30) day period specified in subsection (4) of KRS 211.905 by persons under six (6) years of age, he shall be fined twenty-five dollars ($25).' (KRS 211.994). The current row says only: "orders the owner to remove or cover accessible lead hazards within 60 days, posts the unit unfit for young children if the owner does not". No new row proposed: `edu-lead-state-notices-ky` now states both rules under its own key; the lead may wish to add a pointer sentence to `edu-lead-hazard-release-ky` in a later pass.

KRS 344.362 also exempts 'A landlord who chooses to rent only to men or only to women; provided that the landlord engages in the rental to no more than ten (10) persons or of no more than ten (10) self-contained units in an owner occupied housing accommodation' (KRS 344.362(3)), 'Rooms or rental units where tenants would be required to share common bath or kitchen facilities' (KRS 344.362(4)) and single-sex dormitory rental properties (KRS 344.362(1)). The row's exemption list is not wrong but is incomplete; suggested addition at the next KY sync: "The sex-discrimination ban also does not apply to single-sex dormitory rentals, to rooms or units with a shared bath or kitchen, or to an owner-occupied rental of up to 10 persons or units let to one sex only (KRS 344.362(1), (3), (4))." `edu-student-housing-ky` states it meanwhile.
- (from `tenant-records`) `edu-protected-class-inquiry-ky` (topic protected-class-inquiry-ban) states the bar on using 'a form of application for the purchase, rental, or lease of real property, or to make a record of inquiry' that indicates a limitation by race and other classes (KRS 344.360(6)), but says nothing of the Commission's own data rule: 'The owner of a housing accommodation not exempted by KRS 344.362 or 344. 365 shall list questions regarding the race or national origin of the applicant or lessor on a separate form' (104 KAR 1:060, Section 3(1)); 'The owner may ask for the information, but shall not require the applicant to provide it' (104 KAR 1:060, Section 3(2)); records kept 'for a period of two (2) years' (104 KAR 1:060, Section 4(4)). A landlord reading the row alone may think any race question is barred. Proposed addition (for the lead; the row is not edited here): "Separately, a state regulation directs an owner who is not exempt to ask applicants their race or national origin on a separate form, without requiring an answer, and to keep the answers for two years for the Kentucky Commission on Human Rights (104 KAR 1:060, Sections 3-4; `edu-tenant-records-ky`)." Notes addition: the two quotes above with Read whole: 104 KAR 1:060. No active KY row cites 104 KAR 1:060 (checked by script over the CSV and every saved topic file; `edu-maintenance-duty-shift-ky` names it only as an off-point battery hit).
- (from `utility-allowance-cap`) `edu-utility-submetering-ky` says "No Kentucky statute regulates submetering or billing tenants for utilities" (row text) and its notes say "Public Service Commission regulations (807 KAR) not read". The KAR now in the corpus has a submetering rule: a submetered property exempt from 401 KAR Chapter 8 shall 'Not charge tenants an amount that exceeds' (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2) the tenant share of 'the actual amount charged by the public water system to the owner or operator of a property using a submetered system' (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2), by actual usage in proportion to the whole property's usage, and must designate an operator and certify no cross connections (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2). Electric individual metering is required for new multi-unit residential buildings permitted after May 31, 1981 (807 KAR 5:046, Section 2). The row's sentence is literally true of statutes but misleading. Proposed: add after its first sentence: "A state water regulation does govern water submetering: a property that bills tenants through its own water submeters may charge each tenant no more than the tenant's share of the actual water system charge, based on actual usage in proportion to the whole property's usage, and must designate an operator and certify to the state that the system has no cross connections (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2). New multi-unit residential buildings permitted after May 31, 1981 must have an individual electric meter for each unit, with listed exceptions (807 KAR 5:046)." Notes addition: the two quotes above, and drop "807 KAR not read". Not filed as a new row; the topic is `utility-submetering-disclosure` (main pass or the item 8 helper).
- (from `utility-allowance-cap`) `edu-utility-apportionment-ky` says "No Kentucky statute sets how a landlord may split a shared utility bill among tenants" (row text). For water passed through tenant submeters, 401 KAR 8:020, Section 2 sets the split: each tenant's share of the actual charge, by actual usage 'in proportion to the total amount of water used for the entire submetered property' (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2). Proposed addition: "For water billed through tenant submeters, a state water regulation requires each tenant's charge to be no more than the tenant's share of the actual water bill, based on actual usage in proportion to the whole property's usage (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2); see `edu-utility-allowance-cap-ky`."
- (from `utility-disclosure-attachment`) `edu-utility-submetering-ky` (row text: "No Kentucky statute regulates submetering or billing tenants for utilities, or requires a lease disclosure about it") does not mention the submetered-water owner duty to pass on advisories, public notices and consumer confidence reports: 'Public notices and consumer confidence reports received by the owner or operator pursuant to 401 KAR 8:075 shall be disseminated to property tenants in the next billing period.' (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2(4)); see also 401 KAR 8:020, Section 2(3). This adds to the finding already filed under `utility-allowance-cap`; proposed sentence for that row: "If you bill tenants for water through your own submeters, you must also pass on to tenants any boil water or consumer advisory you receive, and the water system's public notices and consumer confidence reports in the next billing period (401 KAR 8:020, Section 2(3)-(4); `edu-utility-disclosure-attachment-ky`)."
- (from `utility-disconnection-notice-authorization`) `edu-utility-shutoff-ky` (notes: "807 KAR 5:006 not read"; text: "the Public Service Commission's regulations govern regulated utilities") is accurate but now can say what the rule is. 807 KAR 5:006 requires a written notice 'distinguishable and separate from a bill' (807 KAR 5:006, Section 14(5)); for electric or gas nonpayment 'Service shall not, for any reason, be terminated before twenty-seven (27) days after the mailing date of the original unpaid bill' (807 KAR 5:006, Section 15(1)(f)), and for water, sewer or telephone 'Service shall not, for any reason, be terminated before twenty (20) days after the mailing date of the original unpaid bill' (807 KAR 5:006, Section 15(1)(f)); a medical certificate delays termination 'for thirty (30) days beyond the termination date' (807 KAR 5:006, Section 15(2)(c)). Proposed added sentence: "Under the Commission's rules a regulated utility must give its own customer written notice, separate from a bill, before cutting service for nonpayment: 10 days for electric or gas and 5 days for water, sewer or telephone, and never sooner than 27 or 20 days after the unpaid bill was mailed; a medical certificate, and for gas or electric between November 1 and March 31 a Cabinet certificate of eligibility, can delay it 30 days (807 KAR 5:006, Sections 14(5) and 15). These protect the utility's customer; they give a landlord no notice (`edu-no-landlord-shutoff-notice-ky`)." Notes: drop "807 KAR 5:006 not read" and add the quotes above.

### §7 Findings for other states

- `home-business-permission-co` (tagged in several states): its additional-insured term and its damage sentence without a fault limit were declined for Kentucky because a URLTA bar on making the tenant indemnify the landlord's own liability (KRS 383.570(1)(d)) makes them doubtful; other URLTA states with the same bar that tagged it may want the same check.
- Tool note, not a state finding: `kyg_check.py` treats any "battery KYG-..." mention as a citation to resolve; the brief's own wording for a failed run ("battery X recorded as failed, rerun as Y") trips it, so later helpers wrote "run X recorded as failed, rerun as Y". Proposed SOP change below.

### Proposed SOP changes

- **Rule 19 / rule 59 tooling:** a battery-citation checker should resolve only citations in the generated form ("battery ID (search of ...") and skip "recorded as failed, rerun as" mentions; and a KAR loader built from the LRC regulation pages must keep only the engrossed text (the page also prints an "alternate view" of the pre-engrossment text) and drop entries whose own page says "This document is no longer current" (KY-gaps §1: 48 emergency entries listed as Emergency on their chapter pages were expired, inactive, superseded or withdrawn).
- **Rule 26 (helper briefs):** a helper's topic file must carry every tag it names in its log line, with the full segment; the assembler should refuse a log line naming a tag the file doesn't carry (KY-gaps: one file named a tag with no segment).

### Proposed topic questions

- `security-deposit-standards` / `rent-receipts`: Does a real estate commission rule require a broker who manages a rental to escrow deposits and other money, give receipts, or put deposit terms in the lease? (Kentucky, 201 KAR 11:121, Section 10(2)(h)-(i), (4)-(6).)
- `lease-term-limitation`: Does a conveyancing statute require a lease longer than a set term to be made by deed, beyond the statute of frauds? (Kentucky, KRS 382.010.)
- `prohibited-acts-renter`: Does the criminal code make a tenant's damage to the rented property its own offense or grade? (Kentucky, KRS 512.020(1)(a)3.)
- `tenant-records`: Does a fair housing agency rule require owners to keep applicant or resident demographic records, or file an annual report for larger properties? (Kentucky, 104 KAR 1:060.)
- `rights-responsibilities-guide`: Does a fair housing agency rule require landlords to hand out a brochure or display a poster? (Kentucky, 104 KAR 1:010.)
- KAR searches (rule 19): regulation text often lacks landlord or tenant words; for any absence, also search the subject words alone in the state's regulations (Kentucky's checkers found 201 KAR 11:121 rules phrased as "A receipt shall be given for money received").

### New variables

None

## Federal row: disposing of screening reports (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (Washington gaps log §7, backlog item 9): one federal education row, `edu-fcra-disposal-rule`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction, read from the official text (16 C.F.R. part 682 on eCFR; 15 U.S.C. § 1681w on govinfo.gov). It says that anyone who keeps a screening, credit or background report, or a record taken from one, for a business purpose must dispose of it with reasonable measures against unauthorized access (16 C.F.R. § 682.3), with the rule's examples, and that the rule sets no retention period (§ 682.4). This state's existing rows stay; no clause text changed.

## Shared edit: lead-based paint agent's statement (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

From the West Virginia gaps sync (WV gaps log §6 item 21 and §7; backlog items from the Nevada and Puerto Rico syncs): the shared `lead-based-paint` clause gained a bracketed agent's statement and a sentence requiring each person who makes a statement to sign and date it, the two elements the federal rule requires in every lease of pre-1978 housing (40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)(5)-(6); HUD's twin rule, 24 C.F.R. § 35.92(b)(5)-(6); both read on eCFR 2026-11-20). Uniform federal edit under rule 62: the same in every jurisdiction, no state term changed; it applies only where an agent acts for the landlord.

## Model-law comparison: two new topics (Claude Code, 2026-11-21)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20: compare the library's topic list with the uniform model laws (the 1972 Uniform Residential Landlord and Tenant Act, the Revised URLTA of 2015, and UCC § 3-311) and answer any topic they raise that the library lacked. Two did: checks marked 'paid in full' (`paid-in-full-payments`) and a general duty of good faith (`good-faith-obligation`). This jurisdiction's rows are `edu-paid-in-full-checks-ky` (VERIFIED; KRS 355.3-311; KRS 355.1-308(2)) and `edu-good-faith-obligation-ky` (VERIFIED; KRS 383.550; KRS 383.500). Each was researched from official text by an independent agent; every quotation was machine-checked against the saved page and the quotes are in each row's notes. 

### Declined option, 2026-11-21 (Taylor's decision)

- `edu-no-subordination-clause` tagged here and in every jurisdiction: the library offers no clause subordinating the lease to a mortgage (rule 54 row); the tenant's foreclosure protection is the federal PTFA (`edu-ptfa-foreclosure-tenants`).
